Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([46:25](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2785.05)):

And what kind of information were you given upon learning that the patient was coming here? What were you expecting to receive?

Kelly McDonough ([46:35](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2795.16)):

I  was expecting a trauma one response. A patient who had fallen out of a  window from a height, and didn't know much more than that.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([46:45](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2805.6)):

Okay. And so when you say trauma one, what does that mean for you?

Kelly McDonough ([46:49](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2809.23)):

So  we have two designations for trauma responses, a trauma one and a  trauma two, and there's certain criteria for each. And there's a long  list of criteria that are determined by the resource nurse who receives  the EMS call.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([47:00](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2820.6)):

Okay. And so when we say one and two, which is more serious than the other?

Kelly McDonough ([47:05](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2825.13)):

Trauma one.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([47:06](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2826.84)):

Okay.  And so what do you do to prepare when a patient is coming in, and you  know coming in that it's likely a trauma one situation?

Kelly McDonough ([47:15](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2835.96)):

So we gather in the trauma room and make sure that we have all available staff needed to take care of the patient.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([47:21](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2841.42)):

And what kind of staff are with you in the room, in the trauma room?

Kelly McDonough ([47:25](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2845.08)):

So,  for a trauma one response, it would be the emergency medicine  physician. It would be anesthesia, the trauma surgeons. Sometimes  neurosurgery will be there.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([47:34](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2854.8)):

And  fair to say you also have a whole host of other staff, including  nurses, emergency room techs. All of those people are also available to  assist if you need it?

Kelly McDonough ([47:44](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2864.43)):

Correct.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([47:46](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2866.44)):

Now,  when this individual came in, when Lindsay Clancy came into the  emergency department, were you aware from EMS that she had already come  in with some sort of neurological deficits?

Kelly McDonough ([47:59](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2879.1)):

I think that from my best recollection, we knew that she was unresponsive, found down outside of her home.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([48:05](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2885.7)):

Okay. Were you advised that it appeared she might have a spinal injury?

Kelly McDonough ([48:09](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2889.99)):

Yes.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([48:10](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2890.41)):

And that she may have been moving her arms and her head, but not her legs?

Kelly McDonough ([48:15](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2895.09)):

Correct.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([48:16](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2896.86)):

Now,  when the team was assessing her in the emergency department, what types  of steps do you take to perform that assessment of the patient?

Kelly McDonough ([48:25](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2905.53)):

So  there's a series of steps that we go through to assess a trauma patient  that are followed every single time. ABCs: airway, breathing,  circulation, and then we do a secondary survey.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([48:34](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2914.98)):

Okay. And in this circumstance, was the patient breathing?

Kelly McDonough ([48:39](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2919.69)):

Yes.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([48:40](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2920.44)):

And when she came into the emergency department, was she breathing on her own or intubated?

Kelly McDonough ([48:45](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2925.39)):

She was breathing on her own.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([48:47](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2927.1)):

At some point, did the ER staff have to intubate her?

Kelly McDonough ([48:50](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2930.79)):

Yes.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([48:51](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2931.39)):

And why was that?

Kelly McDonough ([48:52](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2932.77)):

For  airway protection. There was a concern that maybe she would have a head  injury and she would decompensate later. And so, to protect her airway,  she was intubated.

Attorney Shanan Buckingham ([49:00](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=2940.87)):

Yes.

I'm sorry?

Can you tell us a little bit about what the crime scene services section is of the Massachusetts State Police?

Rose Stoffers ([01:19:14](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=4754.19)):

Sure.  So we get called to all different kinds of crime scenes. Our main job  is to document the scenes with photos and videos. We can also process  evidence on scene or we collect evidence and bring it back to our lab  and we process it there. We're processing for fingerprints, and then we  do fingerprint analysis, try to identify any fingerprints we find on the  evidence.

The  forensic scientists are civilians who work for the lab, but they will  come out to scenes as well. They do mostly testing of biological  evidence that we don't do. So we work closely with them both at scenes  and they share our office in Lakeville.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:06:44](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11204.94)):

And what is the consultation and liaison service?

Dr. Shah ([03:06:47](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11207.97)):

Yeah.  So consultation liaison psychiatry is a subspecialty of psychiatry that  works at the interface of psychiatry and medicine. So we work both in  the inpatient and outpatient settings. In this particular instance, we  get called by other healthcare providers that may need help with their  patients or have a question related in the psychiatric realm. And so I  would see a patient that's admitted to the hospital and help provide  recommendations to the physicians and the rest of the care team that's  caring for that patient.

So  my team was seeing Ms. Clancy for a number of reasons, including for  psychiatric safety risk assessment. ([03:08:29](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11309.13))
So  that was one of the reasons why I saw her on the 29th is to continue  that assessment. I was also called to ask a new\... Well, I was asked a  new question that day, a consult question on whether she was able to  make the decision to change her healthcare proxy from her, at the time,  husband to her parents.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:08:48](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11328.33)):

And  when you consult to help determine whether someone's capable of making a  decision like that, changing their healthcare proxy, are you evaluating  their medical decision making capacity?

Dr. Shah ([03:09:02](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11342.25)):

That's correct.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:09:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11343.21)):

Okay. How do you go about doing that?

Dr. Shah ([03:09:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11344.77)):

so  there's generally four parts to medical decision making capacity. The  first is whether somebody's able to voice a choice, and whether that  choice is consistent over time. The second is whether they understand  the situation under which they're making that choice. The third part is  appreciation of the risks and benefits. And the fourth part is kind of  being able to reason or rationalize that information to be able to come  to a choice, and how they came to that choice.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:09:35](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11375.49)):

Now,  if you're doing this evaluation to determine someone's capacity to make  decisions, would a traumatic brain injury factor into that evaluation?

Dr. Shah ([03:09:46](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11386.14)):

It could.

Yes.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:09:57](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11397.99)):

And is she speaking to you or writing this down?

Dr. Shah ([03:10:00](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11400.42)):

She's speaking.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:10:01](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11401.29)):

Speaking. So she's already been extubated at that point?

Dr. Shah ([03:10:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11403.66)):

That's correct.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:10:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11404.62)):

And she's speaking to you. And were you aware that Patrick Clancy was her healthcare proxy?

Dr. Shah ([03:10:09](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11409.84)):

I was told that by her primary medical team.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:10:12](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11412.09)):

She did.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:10:18](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11418.12)):

And who was that?

Dr. Shah ([03:10:19](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11419.17)):

Both her parents.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:10:20](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11420.31)):

And you talked about that needing to be a consistent choice. How did you ensure that was a consistent choice with her?

Dr. Shah ([03:10:27](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11427.18)):

Yeah.  So we waited to make the change from Patrick Clancy to her parents  until the next day for a variety of reasons. We wanted to ensure that  that choice was consistent over time. And especially because the day  that I saw her, she had just gone through some pretty major medical  events, including she had a major spine surgery, a decompression and a  fusion.

([03:10:49](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11449.29))
She  had gone through some oxygen desaturations overnight, so was not  breathing as well. Her heart rate was up, all of which then led to her  being what we call delirious. She was confused overnight to the point  where she asked her nurse, "Am I delirious?" And she was also  experiencing some visual hallucinations. So that was one of the reasons  why I was called in to make sure that she was able to make that decision  to change her healthcare proxy.

([03:11:16](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11476.2))
And  then in addition, the team had also voiced concerns that she had come  in after a reported suicide attempt. And so making sure that she was  able to make that decision of sound mind. And so we actually waited  until the next day when she could be reassessed. At that point, her  delirium had cleared, and she was continuing to make that consistent  choice to change her healthcare proxy to her parents.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:11:41](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11501.97)):

And in fact, you noted in the medical records that the hallucinations were part of an issue with oxygen?

Dr. Shah ([03:11:54](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11514.48)):

It  could be. That could be one of the reasons. Delirium is usually due to a  variety of different medical reasons, and oxygen desaturation could be  one of them. She had also just recently had anesthesia related to a  surgery that she had on her spine. So that could be another reason. And  then her heart rate was also actually quite elevated. So that could be  another reason.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:12:16](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11536.2)):

Is  it common for patients who are just coming off of anesthesia from  surgery who have an issue with oxygen and other medical issues that you  described to have visual hallucinations?

Dr. Shah ([03:12:27](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11547.42)):

They  can. It's not that everybody does, but they can. And delirium can be  quite common after surgery, and particularly anesthesia.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:12:37](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11557.08)):

And those hallucinations that she was having, those were resolved by the next day. They were over by the next day, correct?

Dr. Shah ([03:12:43](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11563.41)):

That's correct.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:12:44](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11564.34)):

And her choice to change her healthcare proxy to her parents was consistent the following day, correct?

Dr. Shah ([03:12:49](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11569.41)):

That's right.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:12:50](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11570.94)):

You also talked about the patient has to understand the risks and benefits of the decision, correct?

Dr. Shah ([03:12:56](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11576.25)):

That's correct.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:12:56](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11576.79)):

How do you make sure the patient understands that?

Dr. Shah ([03:12:59](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11579.19)):

Yeah.  So one of the things we do is really ask patients to take us through  their thought process of why they're making that decision, and what led  to them making that decision at that moment in time. So we're able to  kind of see their thought process, and ensure that they're understanding  what it means to remove one person and add another.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:13:18](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11598.48)):

And what was Ms. Clancy's thought process in explaining that to you?

Dr. Shah ([03:13:21](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11601.51)):

Yeah.  She was saying that her parents are quite supportive to her, and that  she was thinking that she would have to change her healthcare proxy from  her husband to somebody else. And we didn't necessarily go into the  reasons as to why she would have to change it, but she explained that  she was very close with her parents. They would see each other  frequently given that they lived in close geographic proximity, and that  she felt that they were very supportive to her and would have her best  interests in mind should we need to invoke the healthcare proxy if she  couldn't make her own medical decisions.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:13:54](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11634.81)):

So was there a concern on her part that her husband wouldn't have her best interests at mind at that point in time?

Mr. Reddington ([03:13:59](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11639.31)):

Objection.

Judge ([03:14:00](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11640.87)):

Sustained.

Comonwealth Attorney ([03:14:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11643.63)):

You  also talked about you have to make sure the patient's thinking about  the information rationally. How do you go about doing that?

Dr. Shah ([03:14:11](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11651.82)):

Yeah.  So one of the things we do is really ensure that the patient  understands the situation and all of the kind of parts around it. So  this comes into play much more when it's about a specific medical  intervention, like an invasive medical procedure or something like an  amputation or a surgery. With a healthcare proxy, one of the things you  want to make sure is that the person that they want to change their  healthcare proxy to, or appoint as their healthcare proxy makes sense in  the context of their life. And Ms. Clancy was able to talk about how  her parents were quite involved in her life. And so that's one of the  things that we would assess.

Sejal Shah ([03:18:00](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=11880.57)):

Most  patients that have either a head injury or severe mental illness, let's  say, just anything going on with the brain retain the ability to make  most medical decisions. And we think about changing healthcare proxies  as a pretty low risk decision. One  of the things when she was admitted... I'm not going to go through all  the stuff we've already talked about with her medical condition and  everything else... you indicated that she was very, very ill, correct?

Sejal Shah ([03:27:29](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12449.73)):

Correct.

Kevin Reddington ([03:27:31](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12451.5)):

Did she appear to be cooperative to you when you would interact with her?

Sejal Shah ([03:27:35](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12455.28)):

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:27:38](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12458.04)):

As a doctor, as a psychiatrist, you're familiar with concept of a bipolar diagnosis, correct?

Sejal Shah ([03:27:45](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12465.48)):

Correct.

Kevin Reddington ([03:27:46](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12466.62)):

Can you tell me what bipolar means?

Sejal Shah ([03:27:48](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12468.24)):

Yeah.  Bipolar disorder is a mood disorder, categorized as a mood disorder  where patients have distinct depressive episodes as well as, depending  on the type of bipolar disorder, either hypomanic episodes or manic  episodes to the point where they don't sleep for days, they still have  lots of energy. There's this decreased need for sleep. There could be  irritability. There can be impulsiveness, racing thoughts. A lot of  patients describe that they're out of control of their behavior. And  then severe episodes can also include psychosis.

Kevin Reddington ([03:28:22](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12502.17)):

And in reference to bipolar, as you were referring to, when somebody is hypomanic, what does hypomanic mean?

Sejal Shah ([03:28:29](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12509.67)):

It's  a lesser degree of mania. When patients are hypomanic, they don't  always experience things like psychosis, but they might experience some  of the other symptoms that I described, including racing thoughts,  moving too quickly, talking too quickly, being irritable.

Kevin Reddington ([03:28:47](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12527.79)):

As  it relates to the hypomania, would that include things such as somebody  who is getting up early in the morning and exercising or cleaning out  their house or selling their property out of a garage and having  difficulty sleeping and they're awake all night?

Sejal Shah ([03:29:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12543)):

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:29:06](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12546.51)):

As  a psychiatrist, one of the things you were concerned about when you  first started to deal with her, as well as when you were releasing her,  if you will, is the medication that she had on board, so to speak, when  she came into the hospital, right?

Sejal Shah ([03:29:19](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12559.71)):

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:29:21](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12561.21)):

I  know it's difficult to recall offhand, but do you recall the  medications that she was actually under from purposes of taking her  blood when it was tested?

Sejal Shah ([03:29:32](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12572.4)):

When she first came to the hospital?

Kevin Reddington ([03:29:33](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12573.96)):

Yeah, if you know.

Sejal Shah ([03:29:35](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12575.85)):

I  believe her medication list previous to the incident, she was on  amitriptyline, diazepam. I believe she was also prescribed lorazepam,  and there may have been one other.

Kevin Reddington ([03:29:51](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12591.57)):

Looking  at that, if I tell you that there was a toxicology report done on her  as of January 24th by way of taking her blood, you're familiar with  that, right, when they analyze for toxicology? And toxicology obviously  would tell us what the medications, if any, what type of drugs she had  on board, booze, cocaine, anything, right?

Sejal Shah ([03:30:13](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12613.8)):

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:30:15](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12615.51)):

And  you're aware that the toxicology report, as well as the report from the  Department of State Police, as well as the NMS Laboratory, which would  be in Pennsylvania, so that's an international or a national respected  laboratory, isn't it, the NMS Lab, if you know?

Sejal Shah ([03:30:34](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12634.71)):

I don't know.

Kevin Reddington ([03:30:36](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12636.39)):

Sejal Shah ([03:30:45](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12645.9)):

Yes.  I believe I found that out later. I did not know that the first time I  saw her because those labs take a little bit of time to come back.

Kevin Reddington ([03:30:52](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12652.17)):

You treated her for about a month, so you were pretty close to-

That's right.

Kevin Reddington ([03:30:54](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12654.45)):

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:30:56](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12656.88)):

Would you agree with me that one of the things that her blood reflected or that she was on was trazodone?

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:31:02](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12662.94)):

Can you tell me what trazodone is?

Sejal Shah ([03:31:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12664.62)):

Yeah.  Trazodone is an antidepressant medication. It's more often utilized as a  sleep aid because one of the side effects is sedation, and so patients  that are often having difficulty sleeping are placed on trazodone.

Kevin Reddington ([03:31:21](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12681.45)):

Trazodone,  does that share some of the qualities of what are referred to as SSRI?  Even though it's not an SSRI, it shares similar qualities?

Sejal Shah ([03:31:32](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12692.31)):

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:31:33](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12693.33)):

Can you tell me what SSRI means?

Sejal Shah ([03:31:35](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12695.64)):

Yeah. It's a selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor, and it's a class of medications that are antidepressants.

Kevin Reddington ([03:31:44](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12704.91)):

The SSRIs, that would also include Zoloft, for example?

Sejal Shah ([03:31:49](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12709.47)):

Correct.

Kevin Reddington ([03:31:50](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12710.28)):

Would also include Prozac, for example?

Sejal Shah ([03:31:53](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12713.04)):

Yes.

Kevin Reddington ([03:31:54](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12714.93)):

Are you as a psychiatrist aware that Zoloft and/or SSRI Prozac should not be prescribed to a patient who has bipolar?

Sejal Shah ([03:32:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12724.74)):

It  can be prescribed. Oftentimes it's prescribed together with another  medication that can be protective because patients with bipolar disorder  may be experiencing depression, and so they may be prescribed an  antidepressant, but along with another medication what we call  protective.

Kevin Reddington ([03:32:21](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12741.09)):

In  reference to the SSRI, the selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor, what  does that mean, selective serotonin reuptake inhibitor? What does that  mean?

Sejal Shah ([03:32:30](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12750.09)):

Oftentimes in depression, patients are lacking serotonin in the spaces in their brain that helps with-

Kevin Reddington ([03:32:38](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12758.37)):

Can I interrupt you? What is serotonin?

Sejal Shah ([03:32:39](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12759.57)):

Yeah.  Serotonin is a neurotransmitter, so it's a hormone in the brain that  works on the brain. It works in other areas in the body as well, but for  these purposes, it works in the brain, at nerve endings, throughout the  body. That neurotransmitter helps with things like mood regulation. For  patients with depression, they're often lacking that ability to have  enough serotonin in those spaces, and so a selective serotonin reuptake  inhibitor prevents serotonin being sucked back into the cell, so it  allows for serotonin to be more available.

Kevin Reddington ([03:33:15](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12795.63)):

What is an SSAI? What does that mean, selective serotonin antagonist?

Sejal Shah ([03:33:22](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12802.41)):

Those are medications that do the opposite.

Kevin Reddington ([03:33:28](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12808.65)):

Correct.

Kevin Reddington ([03:33:38](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12818.61)):

Correct.

Yes.

Yes.

Sejal Shah ([03:33:59](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12839.61)):

It is an antidepressant.

Kevin Reddington ([03:34:01](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12841.65)):

Lamotrigine. Sorry.

Sejal Shah ([03:34:26](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=12866.61)):

Yes.

Well,  I'm a forensic psychiatrist by specialty training and we work in the  courts often doing forensic evaluations of individuals, but I'm also an  adult psychiatrist. And I work at Brigham and Women's Hospital as an  adult psychiatrist on the consult-liaison unit.

Jennifer Sprague ([04:57:56](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=17876.79)):

Sure.  I grew up in the South Shore. I went to Dartmouth College and then I  went to medical school at UMass Chan Medical School. I then did my  psychiatry residency at Harvard Longwood, which consists of Brigham and  Women's, Beth Israel, Boston Children's at the time. And after that, I  did subspecialty training after my psychiatry residency in forensic  psychiatry again at UMass Chan Medical School.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:01:20](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18080.82)):

So  we asked her, "How are you feeling?" " Horrified." And then she wrote  down, "Do I have an attorney?" And I said, "With my team that at this  time, we're just getting to know you and getting to know the case and  we're not sure exactly what is happening." And we asked her, "Do you  have questions for us?" And she asked, "Is my body broken? Are my legs  straight?" And she then asked about can she have visitors and where her  family is.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:02:13](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18133.98)):

It  depends. Psychiatrists are called for all kinds of issues that happen  in the hospital. In this situation, we were asked around diagnostic  clarity, that there was a likely suicide event that occurred. Can you  evaluate her for safety at this time before she goes into surgery?

Jennifer Sprague ([05:02:46](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18166.71)):

Jhilam Biswas ([05:02:57](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18177.51)):

Yes.

Okay. And are you familiar with the characteristics of psychosis?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:03:56](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18236.82)):

Yes.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:03:57](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18237.57)):

Okay. And what are the typical signs or symptoms of someone who is in psychosis?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:04:07](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18247.05)):

It  really depends, and it ranges across a spectrum of symptoms. So we have  a whole category of different types of symptoms that we look at for  psychosis, and they are different for everyone. But they can be what we  call negative symptoms, which are more internal, isolating, not  responding, not reacting very much. And then we have symptoms, like  positive symptoms, that's what we call them in psychiatry, but they're a  little bit more outward focused where objectively you can see what  those symptoms are, like being paranoid, darting your eyes around,  responding to internal stimuli, which means you're responding to voices  and being within a delusion. So there's all kinds of symptoms and they  look different for each and every person.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:05:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18304.05)):

In your conversation with Ms. Clancy, did you notice any internal or external signs of psychosis?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:05:12](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18312.72)):

At the time I documented that I did not note those.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:05:17](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18317.52)):

Okay.  And did you also note that her thinking was linear, goal oriented,  thoughtful, and she didn't appear to be responding to internal stimuli?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:05:30](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18330.99)):

Yes, that is typical language in psychiatric mental statuses, and I did document that.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:05:36](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18336.03)):

What is internal stimuli?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:05:38](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18338.73)):

Oftentimes  it's very difficult for our patients to be able to articulate what  they're hearing. They may have perceptual disturbances, like  hallucinations or auditory hallucinations and maybe hearing voices. When  it's really active, we often see those individuals interacting with  those voices. I didn't see that in that particular moment.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:06:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18363.24)):

So  for example, you could see someone engaging in a ... looks like they're  engaging in a conversation with someone or it looks like they're  looking over at something, thinking something or someone is there when  they're not?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:06:15](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18375.87)):

Correct. That's what responding to internal stimuli is, yes.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:06:20](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18380.43)):

And you didn't see any of that present with Ms. Clancy?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:06:22](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18382.74)):

Correct.

Jennifer Sprague ([05:06:23](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18383.4)):

Okay.  And would it be fair that some of the obvious signs of psychosis would  be someone whose thoughts are disorganized, that their thinking isn't  linear, that they're not making sense, things like word salad, where  they're just throwing in words, making sentences that don't make sense?

Jhilam Biswas ([05:06:47](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=18407.28)):

So  you described symptoms that we can see in people with serious mental  illness who have a psychotic disorder with active symptoms.

Hypomania  is sort of the gradual increase towards mania, the symptoms that we see  in somebody who might have bipolar disorder. And with bipolar disorder,  we see mixed depression, and we see mania, and we see symptoms that  kind of gradually increase to mania, and we call that period hypomania.

I was assigned to the Detective Bureau.

So if I draw your attention to January 24th of 2023, were you working working that day or were you called in?

Robert Flynn ([05:27:02](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19622.37)):

Both. I worked the 8:00 AM to 4:00 PM shift, and I was called in later after my shift.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:27:07](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19627.44)):

Okay. So you became aware of an incident that occurred in the town of Duxbury, approximately 6:00 PM that evening?

Robert Flynn ([05:27:13](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19633.86)):

Yes, ma'am.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:27:14](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19634.61)):

And so when you come back in after working that day shift, where do you go or where are you directed to go?

Robert Flynn ([05:27:22](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19642.5)):

I was contacted by Sergeant Detective Jamali, who informed me to respond to 47 Summer Street.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:27:27](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19647.48)):

And so while you're en route to that location, are you monitoring the radio?

Robert Flynn ([05:27:32](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19652.46)):

Yes, ma'am.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:27:33](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19653.06)):

And are you monitoring communications with other Duxbury officers?

Robert Flynn ([05:27:37](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19657.47)):

Yes,  ma'am. I had a department issued vehicle that I took home and I  responded to it in that vehicle and I had scan on, which is getting all  surrounding agencies, including fire, Pembroke, Kingston, Marshfield.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:27:52](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19672.83)):

And while you were en route and had that radio on, could you hear officers screaming and it being chaotic?

Robert Flynn ([05:28:02](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19682.13)):

Yes, ma'am.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:28:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19683.84)):

When you arrived at 47 Summer Street, were there already other first responders there?

Robert Flynn ([05:28:09](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19689.03)):

Yes, ma'am.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:28:09](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19689.63)):

And  is it fair to say that when you actually arrived at that location, you  were made aware that the children that were on scene had already been  transported?

Robert Flynn ([05:28:21](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19701.39)):

No.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:28:22](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19702.5)):

Okay. So what did you know when you arrived? What did you learn?

Robert Flynn ([05:28:25](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19705.95)):

I  knew that there was an incident involving children. I learned that when  I initially responded and spoke to Sergeant Homestead. He was the one  that informed me that it appeared that a mother had killed her three  kids.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:28:40](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19720.59)):

Okay. When you got there on the scene, were the children still in the house?

Robert Flynn ([05:28:44](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19724.31)):

So  I was under the impression that they were still in the house because  most incidents I've had involving a sudden death at a house, the fire  department declares them on scene and it becomes a police matter and we  help with the investigation.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:28:59](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19739.25)):

Okay.  And that was your understanding based on the information that you had,  correct? But did you come to learn that the children had been  transported?

Robert Flynn ([05:29:07](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19747.59)):

Yes, ma'am.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:29:08](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19748.4)):

And  how about the individual, the fourth person, if you will, on scene?  Were you advised that there was a woman who had been transported from  the scene as well?

Robert Flynn ([05:29:19](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19759.38)):

Yes, ma'am.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:29:21](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19761.87)):

Okay. And as a result of kind of the nature of what you believed you were coming into, did you call for extra help?

Robert Flynn ([05:29:30](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19770.24)):

Within our own department or?

Shanan Buckingham ([05:29:31](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19771.92)):

With an outside department.

Robert Flynn ([05:29:33](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19773.48)):

Yes.  Yeah. So the Massachusetts State Police has jurisdiction over any death  investigation in town of Duxbury and other communities. So we are to  notify investigators of the State Police.

Shanan Buckingham ([05:29:48](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19788.21)):

And  as a detective with the Duxbury Police, you're familiar with that  relationship with the state police that oftentimes when a death  investigation occurs, that you work in connection with the State Police  detectives unit of that jurisdiction to conduct an investigation,  correct?

Robert Flynn ([05:30:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19803)):

Yes, ma'am.

Ms. Sprague ([05:30:00](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19800.06)):

... of that jurisdiction to conduct an investigation, correct?

Speaker 20 ([05:30:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19803.39)):

Yes, ma'am.

Ms. Sprague ([05:30:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19804.86)):

And  so in this instance, when you went there, you believed that the  children had already passed, so you made that phone call to the  detective's unit, correct?

Speaker 20 ([05:30:11](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19811.31)):

That's correct.

Ms. Sprague ([05:30:12](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19812.42)):

And  certainly... Well, sometime thereafter you were advised of the  children's passing and that this was a death investigation, correct?

Speaker 20 ([05:30:20](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19820.79)):

That is correct.

Ms. Sprague ([05:30:22](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19822.41)):

Once  the children had been removed from the scene and the defendant had been  removed to the scene, did you and your other officers from the Duxbury  Police Department do anything to secure the scene?

Speaker 20 ([05:30:32](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19832.61)):

Yes.  Once we gathered more information and I notified the Massachusetts  State Police, we decided to conduct a security sweep of the house. This  is to make sure that there was no further suspects or further victims or  any other people in the house that we may have missed.

Ms. Sprague ([05:30:50](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19850.22)):

In  regards to this security sweep, what is the goal... You just described  it's to kind of identify if there are other people, but as far as your  observations and your contact within the house, do you try to minimize  your contact within the house in those instances?

Speaker 20 ([05:31:05](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19865.79)):

Yes. I'm not searching for any type of evidence. I just have different observations that I obviously notice.

Ms. Sprague ([05:31:11](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19871.85)):

And what areas of the house did you go into prior to the arrival of the state police?

Speaker 20 ([05:31:18](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19878.09)):

I  first entered... I entered with three of us, Sergeant Potrykus, who's  from the Kingston Police Department, and it was Lieutenant Weiler at the  time. So we all went into our own separate rooms and separate ways  throughout the house, but I think ultimately all three of us went to all  three floors of the home.

Ms. Sprague ([05:31:36](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19896.45)):

Okay. And so talking first about the main floor of the house, did you go into the kitchen area?

Speaker 20 ([05:31:43](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19903.17)):

Yes, I did.

Ms. Sprague ([05:31:43](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19903.98)):

And did you make any observations in that kitchen area?

Speaker 20 ([05:31:49](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19909.41)):

It  was clear to me that there was young children living there. There was  some paintings on the wall. I noticed a takeout food bag. The only word I  remember from that is it being Mediterranean written on the receipt.

Ms. Sprague ([05:32:03](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=19923.6)):

Cory Melo ([06:18:15](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22695.03)):

Yes.

Mr. Reddington ([06:18:16](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22696.26)):

Noting  further in separate occasion, "He's still a very happy baby despite his  sleep training. Why I feel guilty is not really hitting his milestones,  not rolling over, although he almost is! And he's babbling, which is a  new milestone." She wrote that, right?

Cory Melo ([06:18:31](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22711.77)):

Correct.

Mr. Reddington ([06:18:32](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22712.22)):

She  wrote, "I have crazy brain fog. I feel like I can't make a plan. I  can't carry it out, like I just live moment to moment waiting for the  next nap time. I'm terrified of Cal getting over tired now, because I  feel I can't help him." She wrote that, right?

Cory Melo ([06:18:46](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22726.92)):

Correct.

Mr. Reddington ([06:18:47](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22727.67)):

She  then wrote, "I'm really worried about going back to work and not being  able to function with the brain fog. I feel like I should start with a  four-hour shift and see how I do. I can't tell if I am withdrawing from  Ativan and Benadryl or is this my new baseline? I feel completely  disconnected with my baby. I feel like I'm going through the motions  every day." She wrote that, right?

Cory Melo ([06:19:09](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22749.36)):

Correct.

And  there were a number of other entries that you put in your police report  about that. One of which was that, "I feel horribly guilty about my  marriage. I want to connect with Pat again. I know he needs it. I need  it. I'm terrified, because I'm terrified of the relationship." She needs  to get birth control first. She made reference to her inability to have  conjugal relations with her husband, right?

Cory Melo ([06:19:34](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22774.53)):

I believe so.

Mr. Reddington ([06:19:36](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22776)):

She  indicated further on into her travels, "I am completely overwhelmed  trying to take care of the kids. I'm drowning every day. I feel guilty  that I'm spending my family's money and not making any. I feel like I  spend way too much." She wrote that, right?

Cory Melo ([06:19:49](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22789.56)):

Correct.

Mr. Reddington ([06:19:51](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22791.18)):

"I'm  terrified of Callan getting sick. He's a sick baby. A sick baby is  miserable. He's sick now with a double ear infection. Clearly has a  bellyache. It's miserable. I feel like the other kids are going to get  him sick all winter long." She wrote that, right?

Cory Melo ([06:20:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22804.23)):

Correct.

Mr. Reddington ([06:20:04](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22804.77)):

"I  always feel strongly against sleep training. I feel like it's abusive  to let the baby cry and not respond, but I made the decision. I did it  with Cal. It is way too much crying. It's incredibly stressful." She  wrote that, right?

Cory Melo ([06:20:17](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22817.61)):

Correct.

Mr. Reddington ([06:20:17](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22817.97)):

"He's  okay. He will be okay. It's not harmful in any way. The doctor said it  was okay. Plenty of people sleep train their kids at that age. It's  necessary as a third child," is what she wrote, right?

Cory Melo ([06:20:28](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22828.74)):

"  I feel incredibly sad and guilty about not breastfeeding anymore. That  was the one thing I could do to help his immune system. And I can tell  he really doesn't like the formula. I'm very sad about this. I know at  this time it's what I needed to do for my mental health." She wrote  that, right?

Cory Melo ([06:20:47](https://www.rev.com/app/transcript/NmE3MWNiZTFhNjZmN2RkZjg4YTQ3NzkxM3I4RlVZNTZCWVVT/o/VEMwMTg3Mjg4MjU3?ts=22847.46)):

I desperately want to go  back to work, but now I don't know how I'm going to function and it  worries me." Wrote that, right?

Correct.
